text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/research-lsatlab-quantifiers.md\n+# LSAT Lab quantifiers: complete caption review\n+\n+Research date: 2026-10-01. [Quantifiers](https://app.lsatlab.com/video-library/?videoid=33015), Matt Sherman, 2026-08-27. Vimeo 1221968872; observed runtime 83:36.320. Caption source: `raw/sources/lsat-lab-lectures/33015/source.en-x-autogen.vtt`. Source-derived teaching summary and ledger below are deliberately concise. Later analysis and examples are original.\n+\n+## Teaching and training in presentation order\n+\n+This class assumes conditional knowledge, introduces quantified language, develops combination patterns with counterexamples, then trains transfer through three polled questions and student explanations. Feedback addresses direction, answer strength, scope, and efficient diagramming. A student requests a separate translation worksheet; the instructor reports none available.\n+\n+| Interval | Teaching focus |\n+|---|---|\n+| 00:00–02:54 | All/most/some; ranges; direction; reversibility |\n+| 02:54–06:56 | Indicators; not-all; few versus a few |\n+| 06:56–10:48 | Plurality; language interpretation |\n+| 11:03–16:58 | Some+some; some+most; three most+most configurations; countermodels |\n+| 16:58–21:39 | Questions; concrete majority-overlap example |\n+| 21:52–28:45 | Some+all; poll; common-term location |\n+| 28:53–33:16 | Most+all; retained versus weakened quantifier |\n+| 33:24–35:14 | All+all; pattern recap |\n+| 35:17–50:07 | Application 1: negated categories; contraposition; pairwise inferences; answer elimination |\n+| 50:07–52:11 | Worksheet request; next-question setup |\n+| 52:11–67:55 | Application 2: mixed quantifiers; reversal; diagram overinterpretation; amounts; subject scope |\n+| 67:59–81:45 | Application 3: selective diagramming; relevant majority pair; strength elimination |\n+| 81:51–83:28 | Frequency guidance; closing |\n+\n+## Original analysis: what LSAT U should learn from the design\n+\n+The chronology supports Joshua's decision to place quantity after conditionals: the class relies on arrow direction, contraposition, common terms, and valid chains before adding quantified relationships. It does not separately remediate these prerequisites.\n+\n+Training demand progresses from identifying a statement's strength to testing whether a proposed connection is forced. Numerical or spatial countermodels provide a reason for failure, rather than merely teaching a list of permitted patterns. Live polls expose disagreement; students then explain their reasoning while the teacher diagnoses it. This creates richer feedback than displaying the correct letter alone.\n+\n+The first application requires combining negative categories with a quantified existence statement. The second exposes errors in reversing strength, importing an unasserted region into a drawn set, comparing counts from percentages, and omitting the restrictive part of a subject. The third rewards reading the entire stimulus before drawing, because several relationships can be irrelevant to the required inference. These distinct demands should become independently assignable LSAT U skills, followed by mixed practice.\n+\n+The observed worksheet request reveals a potential packaging gap in this class, not proof that the platform has no quantity activities. Independent learners need short translation and inference drills before complex question transfer, plus fresh feedback after an error. A class recording can demonstrate reasoning but does not itself supply that practice loop.\n+\n+## Original accuracy checks before adaptation\n+\n+- **Most means greater than 50%, not a minimum of 51%.** A group can have 50.1% possessing a property. Use exact thresholds in teaching and assessment.\n+- **Some means at least one, not at least 1%.** One member of a large group may be far below 1%. Keep counts and proportions distinct.\n+- **All does not by itself assert existence in ordinary formal conditional reasoning.** From all A are B and all A are C, a B/C overlap requires an A to exist. The class's infer-down and shared-antecedent all/all explanations omit that premise. In its first application, a separate some statement establishes relevant existence; that can repair an inference in that context, but not the unconditional general rule.\n+- **Most entails some; it is not equivalent to some.** A momentary equality-style caption should not become course wording.\n+- **No new cross-term inference is not no inference of any kind.** Premises can still yield restatements, existential weakening, or reversals of some. Scope the pattern rules to their intended new endpoint connection.\n+- **A some/most prohibition needs scope qualifications.** Nested subgroup statements can encode additional information. General pattern shortcuts assume the specific three-term arrangement under discussion; they must not override explicit subset structure or extra premises.\n+- **Few and context-sensitive adverbs need careful semantics.** A platform's heuristic classification is not proof of a universal lexical equivalence. Teach the weakest warranted claim and distinguish plurality from majority.\n+- **A drawing's blank region does not establish members.** Use marked witnesses or explicit cardinalities when proving overlap or building counterexamples.\n+- Reported test-frequency guidance is the instructor's estimate, not a verified distribution of current tests.\n+\n+## Original examples for our version\n+\n+1. **Same denominator:** of 100 festival volunteers, 60 speak French and 60 speak Arabic. At least 20 speak both. Change the numbers to 51 and 51; the guaranteed intersection becomes much smaller. This trains why the output need not be most.\n+2. **Different denominators:** most photographers are club members, and most club members are cyclists. Construct a larger club containing many cyclists outside the photographer group. This trains why apparent arrow alignment is insufficient.\n+3. **Existence plus universal:** at least one archivist is bilingual; all bilingual archivists can translate the catalog. Identify a witness and carry that witness through the universal rule.\n+4. **Universal without existence:** every purple badge holder is cleared and every purple badge holder is trained. An empty purple-badge group satisfies both. Add an explicit badge holder before concluding overlap exists.\n+5. **Scope:** most night-shift nurses are certified. Keep night-shift in the quantified subject; do not diagram this as most nurses are certified.\n+6. **Strength and quantities:** some river samples are contaminated and most lake samples are contaminated. Neither fact compares the number of contaminated samples without group sizes.\n+\n+Beginner work can isolate meaning, direction, and marked witnesses. Medium can combine a quantified statement with a universal and contrast valid with invalid pairs. Advanced can add negative terms, scope, redundant information, countermodels, and LSAT transfer. General mixed assignments and targeted assignments should coexist at all three levels; Medium and Advanced can optionally be timed.\n+\n+## Caption coverage and limitations\n+\n+All 1,350 numbered cues were read chronologically through the final sign-off; the initial truncated middle was separately reread. First cue starts 00:02.880; final cue ends 83:28.255, leaving 8.065 seconds before the observed player endpoint. There are six gaps greater than 15 seconds: 22:24.828–22:49.228; 23:09.628–23:43.268; 35:49.596–38:43.786; 51:02.770–51:23.610; 52:11.270–55:16.080; 68:18.985–68:39.065. Context suggests poll setup and independent solving; audio silence is not independently verified. Some auto-cues themselves span long intervals with very little text, so gap detection alone cannot establish audio completeness.\n+\n+Automatic captions confuse some with sum, omit visual referents, and include student mistakes that are subsequently corrected. No full audiovisual review, poll screenshot verification, or board capture was performed. No actual question IDs appear in the reviewed captions; none are inferred from topics. This establishes complete review of the available caption stream, not verbatim fidelity or complete coverage of LSAT Lab's quantifier curriculum.\n*** End Patch"));

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 8.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"659fa1","wall_time_seconds":3.4820774,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":949,"output":"\\r\\n## What an existing archived lesson demonstrates\\r\\n\\r\\n[Must Be True, video 33013](https://app.lsatlab.com/video-library/?videoid=33013), Patrick Tyrrell, 2026-08-27, 97 minutes. Evidence comes from the existing local archive and its coverage analysis, not fresh playback. Summary: the opening separates contraposition, connecting rules, and applying a fact. It contrasts valid bridges with rules that share only a result, uses contraposition to reveal hidden links, addresses illegal reversal and negation, and distinguishes guaranteed consequences from frequency claims. Five worked LSAT applications explore requirements, compounds, subject scope, passive wording, and answer strength. Diagramming is presented as useful when it resolves complexity. This establishes substantive conditional transfer within an inference lesson, not a complete standalone conditional curriculum.\\r\\n\\r\\nArchive: `corpus/lectures/logical-reasoning/must-be-true/core/2026-08-27-33013/`. The source-caption archive is complete; full audio fidelity remains unverified. Adaptation corrections are recorded separately in its coverage ledger.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Teaching progression we can infer, with limits\\r\\n\\r\\nThe library places conditional reasoning among reusable argument patterns, then organizes applications by question family. The archived MBT class demonstrates movement from explicit operations to worked LSAT applications. A useful LSAT U progression is therefore meaning and translation → valid transformations → rule linking versus fact-triggering → application. This is an LSAT U design inference, not a verified timestamp-by-timestamp outline of LSAT Lab\'s Conditional Logic lesson.\\r\\n\\r\\nTraining evidence is strongest for instructor explanations, contrasting valid/invalid moves, repetition as a fluency goal, and worked LSAT questions. Number of repetitions, student answer interface, automated feedback, difficulty progression, mastery gates, and independent reassessment were not verified in this pass. A video library is not itself evidence of a complete independently guided pathway.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Original LSAT U practice proposals\\r\\n\\r\\nUse one shared rule set to isolate different operations: every certified guide is insured; every insured guide has a permit.\\r\\n\\r\\n- Translate each rule before combining them.\\r\\n- Derive certified → permit without assuming any certified guide exists.\\r\\n- Add the fact that Mira is certified; derive Mira\'s insurance and permit.\\r\\n- Add instead that Mira lacks a permit; use contraposition to infer she is not certified.\\r\\n- Ask whether two rules ending in insured can be connected; require explanation of why a shared destination supplies no bridge.\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are original example proposals. Beginner work can isolate one operation; Medium can require choosing the operation; Advanced can vary language and compound structure. Timing remains optional for Medium and Advanced. Quantifiers belong in the following phase under Joshua\'s requirement, with their own assignable skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Unverified portion and browser limitation\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Conditional Logic detail page exposed a Vimeo player iframe, but player controls were unavailable through the inspected DOM. Subsequent navigation and state reads timed out. No full lesson playback, drill completion, transcript extraction, answer submission, or progress change was intentionally performed. Duration and actual fine-grained lesson content remain unverified. The temporary research tab was closed successfully after these failures.\\r\\n\\r\\nNext useful inspection: the Conditional Logic lesson\'s visible chapters/captions if available; a current advanced conditional recording; a Quantifiers recording; and Study Plan practice links. Capture concise teaching summaries and exercise roles, without copying a complete lesson or question bank.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"552402","wall_time_seconds":4.908072,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":101,"output":"brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum\\\\research-lsatlab-structure.md\\nbrainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum\\\\research-lsatlab-quantifiers.md\\nbrainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum\\\\research-lsatlab-core-class.md\\nbrainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum\\\\research-lsatlab-conditional-lesson.md\\nbrainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum\\\\research-lsatlab-advanced-conditionals.md\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"ea985b","wall_time_seconds":2.7043021,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1595,"output":"\\"\\"\\"Pull authorized LSAT Lab lecture captions/video, normalize captions, extract selected frames.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe player URL must be observed in the selected lesson, not guessed. No cookies,\\r\\ncredentials, or browser internals are read. Existing captures are never overwritten.\\r\\n\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nimport argparse\\r\\nimport hashlib\\r\\nimport html\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nimport re\\r\\nimport subprocess\\r\\n\\r\\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\\r\\n\\r\\ndef run(args):\\r\\n    subprocess.run(args, check=True)\\r\\n\\r\\ndef seconds(stamp):\\r\\n    parts = stamp.replace(\',\', \'.\').split(\':\')\\r\\n    return sum(float(value) * 60 ** power for power, value in enumerate(reversed(parts)))\\r\\n\\r\\ndef normalize(vtt, output=None):\\r\\n    cues = []\\r\\n    for block in re.split(r\'\\\\r?\\\\n\\\\s*\\\\r?\\\\n\', vtt.read_text(encoding=\'utf-8-sig\')):\\r\\n        lines = block.splitlines()\\r\\n        timing = next((i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if \' --> \' in line), None)\\r\\n        if timing is None:\\r\\n            continue\\r\\n        match = re.match(r\'(\\\\S+)\\\\s+-->\\\\s+(\\\\S+)\', lines[timing])\\r\\n        if not match:\\r\\n            raise ValueError(\'Unrecognized caption timestamp\')\\r\\n        text = html.unescape(re.sub(r\'<[^>]+>\', \'\', \' \'.join(lines[timing+1:])))\\r\\n        cues.append({\'start\': seconds(match[1]), \'end\': seconds(match[2]), \'text\': text})\\r\\n    if not cues:\\r\\n        raise ValueError(\'No captions found\')\\r\\n    if output is None:\\r\\n        return cues\\r\\n    result = \'\\\\n\'.join(f\'[{c[\\"start\\"]:.3f} - {c[\\"end\\"]:.3f}] {c[\\"text\\"]}\' for c in cues) + \'\\\\n\'\\r\\n    output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\r\\n    if output.exists() and output.read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\') != result:\\r\\n        raise ValueError(\'Existing normalized transcript differs; preserve as a separate revision\')\\r\\n    output.write_text(result, encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\n    return cues\\r\\n\\r\\ndef main():\\r\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\\r\\n    parser.add_argument(\'--video-id\', required=True)\\r\\n    parser.add_argument(\'--player-url\', help=\'Observed Vimeo embed URL including its existing hash\')\\r\\n    parser.add_argument(\'--subtitle-lang\', default=\'en-x-autogen\', help=\'Observed native caption language (default: en-x-autogen)\')\\r\\n    parser.add_argument(\'--video\', action=\'store_true\', help=\'Retrieve video for local exact-time frame captures\')\\r\\n    parser.add_argument(\'--normalize-to\', type=Path)\\r\\n    parser.add_argument(\'--frames\', type=Path, help=\'JSON array: path (output filename), at_seconds\')\\r\\n    parser.add_argument(\'--screenshots\', type=Path)\\r\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\r\\n    if not re.fullmatch(r\'\\\\d+\', args.video_id):\\r\\n        parser.error(\'video-id must be numeric\')\\r\\n    if not re.fullmatch(r\'[A-Za-z]{2,8}(?:-[A-Za-z0-9]{1,16})*\', args.subtitle_lang):\\r\\n        parser.error(\'subtitle-lang must be a single language identifier, such as en-US or en-x-autogen\')\\r\\n    archive = ROOT / \'raw\' / \'sources\' / \'lsat-lab-lectures\' / args.video_id\\r\\n    archive.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\r\\n    captions = archive / f\'source.{args.subtitle_lang}.vtt\'\\r\\n    media = archive / \'source.mp4\'\\r\\n    common = [\'yt-dlp\', \'--referer\', \'https://app.lsatlab.com/\', \'--no-progress\', \'--retries\', \'3\', \'--fragment-retries\', \'3\']\\r\\n    if not captions.exists():\\r\\n        if not args.player_url:\\r\\n            parser.error(\'player-url needed for initial capture\')\\r\\n        run(common + [\'--skip-download\', \'--write-subs\', \'--sub-langs\', args.subtitle_lang, \'--sub-format\', \'vtt\', \'--output\', str(archive / \'source.%(ext)s\'), args.player_url])\\r\\n    if not captions.is_file() or not captions.stat().st_size:\\r\\n        parser.error(f\'No nonempty captions captured for {args.subtitle_lang}; inspect available native tracks and select --subtitle-lang explicitly\')\\r\\n    try:\\r\\n        cues = normalize(captions, args.normalize_to)\\r\\n    except ValueError as error:\\r\\n        parser.error(str(error))\\r\\n    if args.video and not media.exists():\\r\\n        if not args.player_url:\\r\\n            parser.error(\'player-url needed for initial video capture\')\\r\\n        run(common + [\'--format\', \'bestvideo[height<=1080]/best\', \'--concurrent-fragments\', \'16\', \'--output\', str(archive / \'source.%(ext)s\'), args.player_url])\\r\\n    if args.frames:\\r\\n        if not args.screenshots or not media.exists():\\r\\n            parser.error(\'frames requires screenshots output and downloaded source.mp4\')\\r\\n        args.screenshots.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\r\\n        frames = json.loads(args.frames.read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\'))\\r\\n        time_map_path = args.screenshots / \'frame-times.json\'\\r\\n        time_map = json.loads(time_map_path.read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\')) if time_map_path.exists() else {}\\r\\n        probe = subprocess.run([\'ffprobe\', \'-v\', \'error\', \'-show_entries\', \'format=duration\', \'-of\', \'json\', str(media)], check=True, capture_output=True, text=True)\\r\\n        duration = float(json.loads(probe.stdout)[\'format\'][\'duration\'])\\r\\n        if not isinstance(frames, list):\\r\\n            raise ValueError(\'Frame plan must be an array\')\\r\\n        for frame in frames:\\r\\n            name = frame[\'path\']\\r\\n            if Path(name).name != name or Path(name).suffix.lower() not in (\'.png\', \'.jpg\'):\\r\\n                raise ValueError(\'Frame path must be an image basename\')\\r\\n            stamp = float(frame[\'at_seconds\'])\\r\\n            if not 0 <= stamp < duration:\\r\\n                raise ValueError(\'Frame timestamp outside video duration\')\\r\\n            target = args.screenshots / name\\r\\n            if target.exists():\\r\\n                if name not in time_map or float(time_map[name]) != stamp:\\r\\n                    raise ValueError(f\'Existing frame lacks matching timestamp provenance: {name}\')\\r\\n                continue\\r\\n            run([\'ffmpeg\', \'-hide_banner\', \'-loglevel\', \'error\', \'-ss\', str(stamp), \'-i\', str(media), \'-frames:v\', \'1\', \'-update\', \'1\', \'-n\', str(target)])\\r\\n            if not target.is_file() or not target.stat().st_size:\\r\\n                raise ValueError(f\'Frame extraction produced no image: {name}\')\\r\\n            time_map[name] = stamp\\r\\n            time_map_path.write_text(json.dumps(time_map, indent=2) + \'\\\\n\', encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\n    hashes = {p.name: hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in (captions, media) if p.exists()}\\r\\n    print(json.dumps({\'archive\': str(archive), \'subtitle_language\': args.subtitle_lang, \'sha256\': hashes, \'caption_cues\': len(cues), \'last_caption_end\': cues[-1][\'end\']}, indent=2))\\r\\n\\r\\nif __name__ == \'__main__\':\\r\\n    main()\\r\\n"}'}]
